Angular Framework - Complete Flow Documentation

Angular is a frontend framework used to build single-page applications. The main idea of Angular is: 

User Action
     |
     v
Angular Application
     |
     v
Component
     |
     v
Service
     |
     v
Backend API
     |
     v
Database
     |
     v
Response
     |
     v
Update UI

1. Angular Application Starting Flow

When we start an Angular project, Angular does not directly show the page. Many internal steps happen before the user sees the application. 

Developer Runs Command

        |
        v

ng serve

        |
        v

Angular CLI Starts

        |
        v

Read Project Configuration

        |
        v

Load Dependencies

        |
        v

Compile TypeScript

        |
        v

Build Application

        |
        v

Start Development Server

        |
        v

Browser Opens Application

        |
        v

Load index.html

        |
        v

Execute main.ts

        |
        v

Bootstrap Angular

        |
        v

Load Root Component

        |
        v

Application Ready

Simple Example

Like opening a mobile application: 

Click App Icon

        |
        v

Operating System Loads Files

        |
        v

Application Starts

        |
        v

Home Screen Appears

Angular follows the same concept. The browser starts the application and Angular builds the screen.

Angular Folder Structure and File Responsibilities

The folder structure of Angular is designed to separate different responsibilities. Each folder has a specific job. Components handle UI, services handle logic, assets store static files, and configuration files control how Angular builds the application.

1. Complete Angular Project Structure

angular-project/

│
├── node_modules/
│
├── src/
│   │
│   ├── app/
│   │   │
│   │   ├── components/
│   │   │
│   │   ├── services/
│   │   │
│   │   ├── models/
│   │   │
│   │   ├── guards/
│   │   │
│   │   ├── interceptors/
│   │   │
│   │   ├── pipes/
│   │   │
│   │   ├── directives/
│   │   │
│   │   ├── app.component.ts
│   │   ├── app.component.html
│   │   ├── app.component.css
│   │   └── app.routes.ts
│   │
│   ├── assets/
│   │
│   ├── environments/
│   │
│   ├── index.html/
│   │
│   ├── main.ts
│   │
│   └── styles.css
│
├── angular.json
│
├── package.json
│
├── tsconfig.json
│
└── README.md

2. Root Folder

Purpose

The root folder contains the complete Angular project. All application files, configurations, and dependencies exist inside this folder. 

Angular Project

        |
        |
        +---- Source Code

        |
        +---- Configuration

        |
        +---- Dependencies

        |
        +---- Build Files

3. node_modules Folder

Purpose

Contains all external libraries required by Angular. It is automatically created when npm install runs. 

package.json

      |
      v

npm install

      |
      v

node_modules

      |
      v

Angular Libraries Available

Example

Angular needs libraries like:

Angular Core

Router

RxJS

TypeScript

Zone.js

They are stored inside node_modules.

4. src Folder

Purpose

src contains the actual application source code. Most development happens here. 

src

 |

 +-- Application Code

 |

 +-- Images

 |

 +-- Styles

 |

 +-- Configuration

5. app Folder

Purpose

The app folder contains the main Angular application logic. Components, services, routes, and other Angular features are usually placed here. 

app/

 |

 +-- Components

 |

 +-- Services

 |

 +-- Routing

 |

 +-- Models

 |

 +-- Application Logic

Purpose

 +-- Testing File

   |

   v

Summary

  • Component = Creates the screen
  • Template = HTML display
  • Service = Business logic
  • Model = Data structure
  • Router = Page navigation
  • Guard = Protect pages
  • Interceptor = Control API requests
  • Pipe = Format data
  • Directive = Change HTML behavior
  • Assets = Static files
  • Environment = Configuration values
  • main.ts = Starts Angular
  • angular.json = Controls Angular build

Angular Core Architecture and Internal Working Flow

Angular is built using different layers. Each layer has a specific responsibility. The main idea is to divide the application into UI, logic, data, and communication layers.

1. Angular High-Level Architecture

                 User
                  |
                  v
            Browser Screen
                  |
                  v
            Component Layer
                  |
                  v
            Service Layer
                  |
                  v
          HTTP / Data Layer
                  |
                  v
            Backend Server
                  |
                  v
              Database

Simple Explanation

  • Component controls what the user sees.
  • Service controls application logic.
  • HTTP Client communicates with backend.
  • Backend handles data storage.

2. Angular Application Layers

Angular Application

|
|
+-------------------------+
| Presentation Layer      |
|                         |
| Components              |
| Templates               |
| Directives              |
| Pipes                   |
+-------------------------+

            |

            v

+-------------------------+
| Business Layer          |
|                         |
| Services                |
| Validation              |
| Logic                   |
+-------------------------+

            |

            v

+-------------------------+
| Data Layer              |
|                         |
| HTTP Client             |
| API Communication       |
| Models                  |
+-------------------------+

            |

            v

+-------------------------+
| Backend                 |
|                         |
| Server                  |
| Database                |
+-------------------------+

When Angular runs, the execution happens in a specific order. 

1. Browser Loads index.html

             v

             v

             v

             v

             |

             v

             v

             |

             v

             |

             v

8. Module and Standalone Architecture Flow

Traditional Module Based

App Module

    |

    +---- Components

    |

    +---- Services

    |

    +---- Directives

    |

    +---- Pipes

Standalone Architecture

Component

    |

    +---- Imports Required Features

    |

    +---- Runs Independently

Simple Explanation

Older Angular groups features inside modules. Modern Angular allows components to directly manage their dependencies.

11. Angular Important Internal Modules List

  • Angular Core
    Main framework functionality.
  • Component
    Creates UI screens.
  • Template
    Controls HTML display.
  • Router
    Handles page navigation.
  • Dependency Injection
    Manages object creation.
  • Services
    Contains reusable logic.
  • HTTP Client
    Communicates with APIs.
  • RxJS
    Handles asynchronous data.
  • Signals
    Manages reactive state.
  • Change Detection
    Updates UI when data changes.
  • Zone.js
    Tracks asynchronous events.
  • Forms
    Handles user input.
  • Directives
    Changes HTML behavior.
  • Pipes
    Transforms displayed data.
  • Guards
    Protects routes.
  • Interceptors
    Controls HTTP requests.
  • Resolvers
    Loads data before page opening.

Angular Routing Complete Flow

Routing is the system that decides which component should be displayed when the user changes the URL. Angular uses routing to create a Single Page Application (SPA), where pages change without refreshing the entire browser.

1. Basic Routing Concept

User Changes URL

        |
        v

Angular Router Checks URL

        |
        v

Find Matching Route

        |
        v

Load Component

        |
        v

Display Page

Example

URL:

/users


Router Checks


/users  ---> UserComponent


Display:

User Page

2. Router Main Components

  • Router
    Controls navigation between pages.
  • Routes
    Contains URL and component mapping.
  • Router Outlet
    Place where Angular displays components.
  • Router Link
    Used to navigate from HTML.

3. Routing Structure Flow

app.routes.ts


      |

      v


Route Definition


      |

      v


URL Matching


      |

      v


Component Loading


      |

      v


Router Outlet


      |

      v


Page Display

4. Router Outlet Flow

Router Outlet is an empty container where Angular inserts the selected component. 

App Component


      |

      v


<router-outlet>


      |

      v


Loaded Component


      |

      v


Screen

Example

App Component


|
|
+---- Header
|
|
+---- Router Outlet
        |
        |
        +---- Dashboard Component

5. Navigation Flow

User Clicks Link


        |

        v


Router Link


        |

        v


Router Service


        |

        v


Change URL


        |

        v


Load Component


        |

        v


Update Screen

 |

9. Lazy Loading Flow

Lazy loading loads features only when required. It improves application speed. 

Application Starts


        |

        v


Load Only Home Page


        |

        v


User Opens Admin Page


        |

        v


Download Admin Code


        |

        v


Load Admin Component

Example

Without Lazy Loading:


Open Website


      |

      v


Download Everything


-------------------


With Lazy Loading:


Open Website


      |

      v


Download Required Page Only

10. Route Guard Flow

Guards decide whether navigation is allowed. 

User Requests Page


        |

        v


Router


        |

        v


Guard


        |

        v


Check Permission


        |

        +----------------+

        |                |

        v                v

     Allowed          Blocked


        |                |

        v                v


 Open Page       Redirect Login

Example

User Opens:

/admin


Guard Checks:

Is User Admin?


Yes

   |

   v

Open Admin Page


No

   |

   v

Go To Login

11. Resolver Flow

Resolver loads data before opening a page. 

User Opens Page


        |

Components should handle only UI. API communication should be separated into services.

     |

     v

     |

     v

- Request
- Response
- Error

Angular Advanced Architecture Flow

This section explains how Angular manages large applications internally: modules, standalone components, dependency injection levels, lifecycle, and real project organization.

1. Angular Module Architecture Flow

Angular originally used NgModules to organize application features. A module groups related components, services, directives, and pipes together.

      |

      v

      v

Modern Angular supports standalone components. A component can directly import what it needs without depending on a module.

Django Framework - Complete Flow Documentation

Django is a backend web framework written in Python. It follows the MVT architecture: 

M - Model
V - View
T - Template

Django mainly handles:

  • Receiving browser requests
  • Processing business logic
  • Communicating with database
  • Returning HTML pages or API responses

1. Django High Level Architecture Flow

User Browser

      |
      v

URL Request

      |
      v

Django URL Dispatcher

      |
      v

View

      |
      v

Service / Business Logic

      |
      v

Model

      |
      v

Database

      |
      v

Response

      |
      v

Template

      |
      v

HTML Page

      |
      v

User Browser

Simple Example

User Opens:

example.com/products


Django:

"Which code handles /products?"


URL finds View


View gets products


Model talks to Database


Template displays products


User sees Product Page

2. Django Request Response Flow

Every request in Django follows a fixed path. 

User Requests Page

        |
        v

Web Server

        |
        v

Django Application

        |
        v

Middleware

        |
        v

URL Router

        |
        v

View Function/Class

        |
        v

Business Logic

        |
        v

Model

        |
        v

Database

        |
        v

Response Created

        |
        v

Browser

Example

User:

Open Profile Page


        |

        v


Django receives:

/profile


        |

        v


Profile View runs


        |

        v


Gets User Data


        |

        v


Returns Profile HTML

3. Django MVT Architecture Flow

             USER

              |

              v

          URL ROUTER

              |

              v

            VIEW

              |

       +------+------+

       |             |

       v             v

    MODEL       TEMPLATE

       |             |

       v             |

   DATABASE         |

       |             |

       +------+------+

              |

              v

15. Django Important Modules List

  • Project
    Main application configuration.
  • Application
    Feature-based modules.
  • URL Dispatcher
    Maps URLs to views.
  • Views
    Processes requests.
  • Models
    Database structure.
  • ORM
    Communicates with database using Python.
  • Migration
    Updates database structure.
  • Templates
    Creates HTML pages.
  • Static Files
    CSS, JavaScript, Images.
  • Middleware
    Runs between request and response.
  • Forms
    Handles user input.
  • Authentication
    Login, users, permissions.
  • Admin Panel
    Manage database data.
  • Django REST Framework
    Creates APIs.

Django Project Structure and Application Architecture

Before building a Django application, it is important to understand the difference between a Django Project and a Django Application.

1. Django Project vs Django App

Django Project

A project is the complete website/application configuration. It controls settings, database connection, installed apps, and global URLs.

Django Application

An app is a small module that performs a specific business function. A project can contain many apps. 

Django Project

        |

        +----------------+

        |                |

        v                v

    Users App       Products App

        |                |

        v                v

   Login Logic    Product Logic


        |

        v

     Orders App

        |

        v

    Order Logic

Example

E-Commerce Project


Project:

shopping


Apps:


users

- Login
- Registration


products

- Product Listing
- Product Details


orders

- Cart
- Payment
- Orders

      v


Start Development

Django Models and ORM Complete Flow

Models are one of the most important parts of Django. A model defines how data is stored in the database. Instead of writing SQL manually, Django provides ORM (Object Relational Mapping) to communicate with the database using Python code.

1. Model Basic Concept

Python Class

      |

      v

Django Model

      |

      v

Migration

      |

      v

Database Table

      |

      v

Database Records

name

price

description


        |

        v


Database Table

id | name | price | description

      v

      |

      v

      |

      v


Result

      v

Django Migration Complete Flow

Migration is the process that converts changes in Django models into actual database changes. Whenever we create or modify a model, Django needs to update the database structure.

1. Migration Basic Concept

models.py

      |

      v

Django Detects Changes

      |

      v

Migration File

      |

      v

Database Schema Update

Simple Example

Developer adds field:


Product Model


name

price

image


        |

        v


Migration Created


        |

        v


Database gets new column:


image

2. Why Migration Is Needed?

Django models are Python code, but databases understand tables and columns. Migration acts as a bridge between them. 

Python World


models.py


     |

     v


Migration


     |

     v


Database World


Tables

Columns

Indexes

3. Complete Migration Flow

Developer Changes Model


          |

          v


Run makemigrations


          |

          v


Django Creates Migration File


          |

          v


Review Migration


          |

          v


Run migrate


          |

          v


Django Executes SQL


          |

          v


Database Updated

4. makemigrations Flow

Command:


python manage.py makemigrations


        |

        v


Django Checks Models


        |

        v


Finds Differences


        |

        v


Creates Migration File

Example

Before:


Product

- name


After:


Product

- name

- price



makemigrations

11. Migration Dependency Flow

Migrations run in order because later migrations depend on earlier ones.

      |

      v

      |

      v


0003_add_orders


      |

      v


0004_add_payment

Example

Orders Table


Needs:


User Table


Therefore:


User Migration First

Orders Migration Second

Django Views Complete Flow

Views are the main processing layer in Django. A view receives the user request, performs required logic, communicates with models or services, and returns a response.

1. View Basic Concept

User Request

      |

      v

URL

      |

      v

View

      |

      +----------------+

      |                |

      v                v

 Model             Template/API

      |                |

      v                v

 Database          Response

      |

      v

 User

Simple Example

User opens:


/products


        |

        v


URL finds Product View


        |

        v


View gets products


        |

        v


Return Product Page

2. View Responsibilities

View


 |

 +-- Receive Request

 |

 +-- Validate Data

 |

 +-- Call Service

 |

 +-- Get Model Data

 |

 +-- Process Logic

 |

 +-- Return Response

Important

View should not contain too much business logic. Large applications usually use a service layer. 

Small Project:


View

 |

 v

Model



Large Project:


View

 |

 v

Service

 |

 v

Model

      v

Django Service Layer Complete Flow

The service layer is not a built-in Django requirement. It is a project architecture pattern used in large applications to separate business logic from views.

The main idea: 

View handles:

- Request
- Response


Service handles:

- Business Rules
- Complex Logic
- Multiple Operations


Model handles:

- Database Data

1. Why Service Layer Is Needed?

In small projects, developers often put all logic inside views. As the project grows, views become difficult to maintain.

Without Service Layer

View


 |

 +-- Validate User

 |

 +-- Check Payment

 |

 +-- Calculate Price

 |

 +-- Update Database

 |

 +-- Send Email

 |

 +-- Return Response

Problem: 

Large View

Hard To Read

Hard To Test

Hard To Reuse

With Service Layer

View


 |

 v


Service


 |

 +-- Business Logic

 +-- Calculations

 +-- Rules

 +-- Multiple Models


 |

 v


Model


 |

 v


Database

2. Service Layer Architecture Flow

User


 |

 v


URL


 |

 v


View


 |

 v


Service


 |

 v


Model


 |

 v


Database


 |

 v


Response

Simple Example

User Places Order


        |

        v


Order View


        |

        v


Order Service


Checks:


- Product Available?

- Calculate Total

- Apply Discount

- Create Order


        |

        v


Order Model


        |

        v


Database

3. View vs Service Responsibility

View Service
Receive Request Business Logic
Return Response Calculations
Handle HTTP Database Operations
Call Service Call Models

Django Middleware Complete Flow

Middleware is a layer that works between the user's request and Django's view. It can inspect, modify, or stop requests and responses before they reach the final destination.

1. Middleware Basic Concept

User Request


      |

      v


Middleware


      |

      v


URL Resolver


      |

      v


View


      |

      v


Response


      |

      v


Middleware


      |

      v


User

Simple Example

User Opens Website


        |

        v


Middleware Checks:


"Is user logged in?"


        |

        v


Allow Request


        |

        v


View Runs

2. Why Middleware Is Needed?

Middleware handles common operations that should run for many requests. 

Middleware


 |

 +-- Authentication

 |

 +-- Security Checks

 |

 +-- Logging

 |

 +-- Request Processing

 |

 +-- Response Processing

 |

 +-- Error Handling

Example

Without Middleware:


Every View checks:

Django Authentication and Authorization Complete Flow

Authentication means verifying who the user is. Authorization means deciding what the user is allowed to do.

1. Authentication vs Authorization

Authentication

"Who are you?"


Example:

Login with username and password



Authorization

"What can you do?"


Example:

Admin can delete products

User can only view products

FastAPI Authentication and Security Complete Flow

Authentication is the process of identifying a user. Authorization is the process of checking what that user can access. FastAPI usually implements security using OAuth2, JWT tokens, and dependency injection.

1. Authentication vs Authorization

Authentication


Question:


"Who are you?"



Example:


Username + Password



        |



        v



User Identity





Authorization


Question:


"What can you do?"



Example:


Admin can delete


Customer cannot delete

2. FastAPI Security Architecture

Client


 |

 v


Login API


 |

 v


Verify User


 |

 v


Generate Token


 |

 v


Return JWT


 |

 v


Client Stores Token


 |

 v


Future API Requests


 |

 v


Security Dependency


 |

 v


Validate Token


 |

 v


Protected API

FastAPI Advanced Features Complete Flow

FastAPI provides advanced features for building real-world applications such as background processing, asynchronous operations, real-time communication, file handling, caching, and testing.

1. Async and Await Concept

FastAPI supports asynchronous programming using Python async and await keywords. It allows handling multiple requests efficiently.

 |
 v

 |
 v

Async Request

Laravel Authentication, Authorization and API Complete Flow

Laravel Deployment Complete Flow

Deployment means moving a Laravel application from a developer machine to a production server where real users can access it. A production Laravel system contains a web server, PHP processor, database, queue workers, scheduler, cache system, and monitoring.

Spring Boot Database and Hibernate Complete Flow

Spring Boot uses JPA and Hibernate to communicate with databases. Instead of manually writing SQL for every operation, developers work with Java objects called Entities. Hibernate converts these objects into SQL queries.

Spring Boot REST API and Security Complete Flow

Spring Boot is widely used to create REST APIs. A REST API allows frontend applications, mobile applications, and other systems to communicate with the backend using HTTP requests. Spring Security protects these APIs using authentication and authorization.

Jakarta EE Complete Flow

Jakarta EE (Jakarta Enterprise Edition) is a Java enterprise framework used for building large-scale, secure, and distributed applications. It provides APIs for web applications, databases, messaging, security, transactions, and enterprise services.

Hibernate Complete Flow

Hibernate is a Java ORM (Object Relational Mapping) framework used to communicate between Java applications and relational databases. It converts Java objects into database records and database records back into Java objects.

1. Hibernate Basic Architecture

                 JAVA APPLICATION


                       |


                       v


                  HIBERNATE


                       |


        +--------------+--------------+

        |                             |


        v                             v


   SESSION API                   ORM ENGINE


        |


        v


   JDBC


        |


        v


    DATABASE

2. Hibernate Main Purpose

Without Hibernate:


Java Object


     |


     v


Write SQL Manually


     |


     v


Database



----------------



With Hibernate:


Java Object


     |


     v


Hibernate


     |


     v


Automatically Generate SQL


     |


     v


Database

3. Hibernate Complete Flow

Application


      |


      v


Hibernate Configuration


      |


      v


SessionFactory


      |


      v


Session


      |


      v


Transaction


      |


      v


Entity Object


      |


      v


Database

 |


 +-- Configuration


 |


 +-- SessionFactory

 +-- Session

 +-- Transaction

 +-- Query

Hibernate Advanced Flow

Hibernate internally manages objects, database states, and performance optimization. This section explains how Hibernate handles object lifecycle, automatic updates, fetching, and advanced database operations.

1. Hibernate Entity Lifecycle

Every Hibernate entity moves through different states during its lifetime. 

        TRANSIENT


            |


            v


        PERSISTENT


            |


            v


        DETACHED


            |


            v


        REMOVED

2. Transient State

Transient means the object exists only in Java memory and is not connected with the database. 

Create Object


     |


     v


Java Memory


     |


     v


No Database Record

Example

User user = new User();


Object Created


Not Saved In Database

3. Persistent State

Persistent objects are connected with Hibernate Session and database. Changes are automatically tracked. 

Object


   |


   v


Session


   |


   v


Hibernate Tracking


   |


   v


Database

4. Detached State

Detached objects were connected before but the session is closed. 

Persistent Object


        |


        v


Close Session


        |


        v


Detached Object

5. Removed State

Removed objects are marked for deletion from the database. 

Persistent Object


        |


        v


delete()


        |


        v


Removed From Database

6. Complete Entity Lifecycle Flow

Create Object


      |


      v


Transient


      |


      v


save()


      |


      v


Persistent


      |


      v


close session


      |


      v


Detached


      |


      v


delete()


      |


      v


Removed

7. Persistence Context Concept

Persistence Context is a memory area where Hibernate manages entity objects.

      |


      v

      |


      v

      |


      v

      |


      v


Database

9. Dirty Checking Concept

Hibernate automatically detects changes in managed objects and updates the database. 

Load Object


      |


      v

      |


      v

      |


      v

      |


      v

        v

Eager Loading:

Flutter Complete Flow

Flutter is a UI framework created by Google for building mobile, web, and desktop applications from a single codebase using the Dart programming language.

The main concept of Flutter is: Everything in Flutter is a Widget. The complete application is built by combining widgets into a widget tree.

React Native Complete Flow

React Native is a framework created by Meta for building mobile applications (Android and iOS) using JavaScript or TypeScript. It allows developers to create native mobile apps using reusable components.

The main concept: React Native uses components to create UI, state to control changes, services to communicate with external systems, and native bridges to access device features.

Tailwind CSS Complete Flow

Tailwind CSS is a utility-first CSS framework used to build custom user interfaces by applying small reusable CSS classes directly in HTML, React, Angular, Vue, and other frontend frameworks.

The main concept: Instead of writing many custom CSS files, Tailwind provides utility classes that combine together to create complete designs.

React Complete Flow

React is a JavaScript library created by Meta for building fast and reusable user interfaces. React applications are built using components, state management, hooks, services, and a virtual DOM rendering system.

The main concept: Everything in React is a Component. Components receive data, manage state, handle events, and update the UI.

1. React Basic Architecture

                         USER


                          |


                          v


                    REACT APPLICATION


                          |

          |


          v

          v


Real DOM Updated


          |


          v


UI Displayed

Vue.js Complete Flow

Vue.js is a progressive JavaScript framework used for building user interfaces and single-page applications. Vue applications are built using components, reactive data, directives, state management, routing, and services.

The main concept: Vue uses components as building blocks. Data changes automatically update the user interface through Vue's reactive system.

1. Vue.js Basic Architecture

                       USER


                        |


                        v


                  VUE APPLICATION


                        |


                        v


                   COMPONENT TREE

                        v


                    SERVICES

             |                     |

            API              DATABASE

          v

          v

          |


          v

          |


          v


Display UI

       v

       v

       v

Next.js Complete Flow

Next.js is a React-based full-stack framework created for building modern web applications. It adds features like routing, server-side rendering, API handling, authentication support, database integration, optimization, and deployment features on top of React.

The main concept: React builds components, while Next.js provides the complete application structure and server capabilities around those components.

1. Next.js Basic Architecture

                         USER


                          |


                          v


                    NEXT.JS APPLICATION


                          |


                          v


                    ROUTING SYSTEM


                          |


             +------------+------------+


             |                         |


             v                         v


       SERVER COMPONENTS          CLIENT COMPONENTS


             |                         |


             +------------+------------+


                          |


                          v


                   REACT COMPONENTS


                          |


                          v


                       UI OUTPUT

        |


        v

        |


        v


Fetch Required Data


        |


        v


Render HTML


        |


        v


Send Response To Browser


        |


        v


Display Page

       |


       v

       v

       |


       v

       v

Express.js Complete Flow

Express.js is a lightweight backend framework built on Node.js. It is mainly used to create web servers, REST APIs, middleware systems, authentication systems, and backend applications.

The main concept: A request comes from the client, Express receives it through routes, processes it using middleware and controllers, communicates with services and databases, then sends a response back.

1. Express.js Basic Architecture

                         CLIENT


                           |


                           v


                     HTTP REQUEST


                           |


                           v


                    EXPRESS SERVER


                           |


                           v


                     MIDDLEWARE


                           |


                           v


                      ROUTER


                           |


                           v


                    CONTROLLER


                           |


                           v


                     SERVICE


                           |


                           v


                     DATABASE


                           |


                           v


                    HTTP RESPONSE

        |


        v

        |


        v


Express Application Starts Processing


        |


        v

        |


        v

        |


        v


Controller Handles Logic


        |


        v


Service Performs Operation


        |


        v


Database Communication


        |


        v


Response Sent To User

       |


       v

       |

NestJS Complete Flow

NestJS is a Node.js backend framework built with TypeScript. It is used for creating scalable, maintainable, and enterprise-level server applications.

The main concept: NestJS organizes backend code using Modules, Controllers, Services, and Dependency Injection. A request enters through a Controller, business logic is handled by Services, and data is managed through repositories or databases.

1. NestJS Basic Architecture

                         CLIENT


                           |


                           v


                    HTTP REQUEST


                           |


                           v


                    NESTJS SERVER


                           |


                           v


                       MODULE


                           |


                           v


                    CONTROLLER


                           |


                           v


                     SERVICE


                           |


                           v


                  REPOSITORY / MODEL


                           |


                           v


                     DATABASE


                           |


                           v


                    RESPONSE

2. NestJS Application Flow

User Sends Request


        |


        v


NestJS Server Receives Request


        |


        v


Middleware Executes


        |


        v


Guard Checks Permission


        |


        v


Controller Receives Request


        |


        v


Service Executes Business Logic


        |


        v


Database Operation


        |


        v


Response Returned

3. NestJS Project Creation Flow

Install Node.js


       |


       v


Install Nest CLI


       |


       v


Create Nest Project


       |


       v


Generate Modules


       |


       v


Create Controllers


       |


       v


Create Services


       |


       v


Connect Database


       |


       v


Run Application

Flask Complete Flow

Flask is a lightweight Python web framework used for building web applications, REST APIs, backend services, and small to medium scale applications.

The main concept: A request comes from the user, Flask receives it through routes, executes view functions, processes business logic using services, communicates with models/database, and returns a response.

1. Flask Basic Architecture

                         CLIENT


                           |


                           v


                    HTTP REQUEST


                           |


                           v


                     FLASK SERVER


                           |


                           v


                      ROUTES


                           |


                           v


                       VIEWS


                           |


                           v


                     SERVICES


                           |


                           v


                      MODELS


                           |


                           v


                    DATABASE


                           |


                           v


                    RESPONSE

2. Flask Application Flow

User Opens Website


        |


        v


Browser Sends Request


        |


        v


Flask Server Receives Request


        |


        v


URL Router Finds Route


        |


        v


View Function Executes


        |


        v


Business Logic Runs


        |


        v


Database Operation


        |


        v


Response Returned


        |


        v


Browser Displays Result

Install Python


       |


       v

       |


       v


Install Flask


       |


       v

       |


       v


Create Routes


       |


       v


Connect Database


       |


       v
